## 3.3 Evaluation Measures

We follow the CapMAS dual protocol (Lee et al. 2025). Precision (factuality) decomposes a caption into atomic propositions and reports the fraction a multimodal judge finds supported by the image and IIW reference description. Recall (covera<sub>g</sub>e) is the fraction of human-verified, ima<sub>g</sub>e-derived <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e-c</sub>h<sub>o</sub>i<sub>ce ques</sub>ti<sub>ons answere</sub>d <sub>correc</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e cap-</sub> ti<sub>on a</sub>l<sub>one, w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>mage w</sub>ithh<sub>e</sub>ld<sub>.</sub> F1 i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> h<sub>armon</sub>i<sub>c</sub> mean. ## 4.2 SimLoss Fully Diferentiable Fine-Tuning (FFT)

We call the diferentiable projector-alignment variant Sim-Loss FFT. Here, fully diferentiable describes the training <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l<sub>,</sub> <sub>no</sub>t f<sub>u</sub>ll<sub>-parame</sub>t<sub>er</sub> t<sub>un</sub>i<sub>ng:</sub> <sub>gra</sub>di<sub>en</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>pass</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h th<sub>e</sub> projector into the LoRA adapters, while the base weights and <sub>em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ng</sub> <sub>enco</sub>d<sub>er</sub> <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>n</sub> f<sub>rozen.</sub> F<sub>or</sub> <sub>eac</sub>h i<sub>mage,</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>com-</sub> pu<sup>te</sup> $z _ { i } ^ { I }$ <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>oca</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>access</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> f<sub>rozen</sub> i<sub>mage</sub> <sub>enco</sub>d<sub>er</sub> <sub>an</sub>d $z _ { i } ^ { V }$ f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> VLM hidd<sub>en</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es,</sub> th<sub>en</sub> <sub>op</sub>ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>ze</sub> E<sub>qua</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 11 <sub>over</sub> i<sub>n-</sub>b<sub>a</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h <sub>pos</sub>iti<sub>ves an</sub>d <sub>nega</sub>ti<sub>ves.</sub> D<sub>ur</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> projector and frozen encoder are removed, leaving the origi-<sub>na</sub>l <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e-pass s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ure.</sub>

## 4.3 SimLoss GRPO with Black-Box Embeddings

The projector-based SimLoss objective requires access to the <sub>em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ng mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l’<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erna</sub>l <sub>compu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on grap</sub>h<sub>.</sub> Wh<sub>en</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ng</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l i<sub>s</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ose</sub>d<sub>-source</sub> <sub>or</sub> <sub>access</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h <sub>an</sub> API<sub>,</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>t b<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>propaga</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s no</sub>t <sub>poss</sub>ibl<sub>e.</sub> T<sub>o</sub> h<sub>an</sub>dl<sub>e</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> <sub>se</sub>tti<sub>ng, we</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne a rewar</sub>d<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t<sub>,</sub> Si<sub>m</sub>L<sub>oss</sub> GRPO<sub>.</sub> Given an image v and a sampled caption $\hat { y } \sim \pi _ { \boldsymbol { \theta } } ( \cdot \mid x , v )$ <sub>we enco</sub>d<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>mage an</sub>d t<sub>ex</sub>t <sub>us</sub>i<sub>ng a</sub> bl<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ox mu</sub>lti<sub>mo</sub>d<sub>a</sub>l <sub>em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ng mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>:</sub>

$$
\begin{array} { r } { z ^ { I } = E _ { I } ( v ) , \qquad z ^ { T } = E _ { T } ( \hat { y } ) . } \end{array}
$$

W<sub>e</sub> th<sub>en</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub> th<sub>e</sub> Si<sub>m</sub>L<sub>oss</sub> <sub>rewar</sub>d <sub>as</sub>

(12)

$$
r _ { \mathrm { S i m } } ( v , \hat { y } ) = \cos ( z ^ { I } , z ^ { T } ) .\tag{13}
$$

## 6.2 Main Results

The stron<sub>g</sub>est m<sub>e</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s a</sub>r<sub>e</sub> C<sub>ap</sub>MAS <sub>a</sub>nd SimL<sub>oss</sub> FFT<sub>.</sub> C<sub>ap</sub>MAS <sub>o</sub>bt<sub>a</sub>in<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h<sub>es</sub>t F1 <sub>score,</sub> 0<sub>.</sub>7025<sub>,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> Si<sub>m</sub>L<sub>oss</sub> FFT <sub>reac</sub>h<sub>es</sub> 0<sub>.</sub>7023<sub>, o</sub>nl<sub>y</sub> 0<sub>.</sub>0002 l<sub>owe</sub>r<sub>.</sub> SimL<sub>oss</sub> FFT <sub>a</sub>l<sub>so ac</sub>hi<sub>eves</sub> th<sub>e</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h<sub>es</sub>t <sub>prec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>among</sub> <sub>a</sub>ll <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s,</sub> 0<sub>.</sub>8485<sub>,</sub> <sub>s</sub>li<sub>g</sub>htl<sub>y</sub> <sub>ex-</sub> <sub>cee</sub>din<sub>g</sub> C<sub>ap</sub>MAS <sub>a</sub>t 0<sub>.</sub>8467<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>ese</sub> r<sub>esu</sub>lt<sub>s suppo</sub>rt <sub>ou</sub>r <sub>ce</sub>n<sub>-</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>l <sub>c</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>m:</sub> i<sub>mage-em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ng</sub> <sub>superv</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>can</sub> <sub>a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t <sub>a</sub> fi<sub>ne-</sub> <sub>gra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>cap</sub>ti<sub>oner</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>groun</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>ru</sub>th <sub>or</sub> <sub>p</sub>i<sub>pe</sub>li<sub>ne-genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>cap</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t<sub>s w</sub>hil<sub>e recover</sub>i<sub>ng near</sub>l<sub>y a</sub>ll <sub>o</sub>f C<sub>ap</sub>MAS’<sub>s</sub> F1 b<sub>ene</sub>fit i<sub>n</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>pass.</sub>

SimLoss FFT gives the best precision with the shortest captions. SimLoss FFT improves precision from 0.7884 to 0.8485 while cutting mean caption length from 347 words to 114, with a standard deviation of 13 against the baseline’s 46. This admits a stronger reading than “shorter is not worse.” H<sub>o</sub>ldi<sub>ng groun</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>l <sub>con</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t <sub>approx</sub>i<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>e</sub>l<sub>y</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d<sub>, as</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fl<sub>a</sub>t <sub>reca</sub>ll <sub>con</sub>fi<sub>rms</sub> it i<sub>s, a cap</sub>ti<sub>on convey</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>con</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t i<sub>n</sub> f<sub>ewer wor</sub>d<sub>s</sub> i<sub>s a more e</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>enco</sub>di<sub>ng o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>mage.</sub> This is consistent with the objective: it encourages the inter-<sub>na</sub>l <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>o re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t id<sub>en</sub>tifi<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e source</sub> i<sub>mage, ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> t<sub>o repro</sub>d<sub>uce a ver</sub>b<sub>ose re</sub>f<sub>er-</sub> <sub>ence or op</sub>ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>ze</sub> f<sub>or</sub> l<sub>eng</sub>th<sub>-corre</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>rewar</sub>d<sub>s.</sub> I<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e no</sub>i<sub>sy-</sub> channel terms of Section 3.4, the model preserves I(v; ˆy) <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> <sub>spen</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> f<sub>ewer</sub> <sub>sym</sub>b<sub>o</sub>l<sub>s,</sub> t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>mm</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>gener</sub>i<sub>c</sub> <sub>p</sub>h<sub>ras</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>l <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence.</sub>

SimLoss GRPO improves recall but is less precise. Why do reward-style baselines underperform? FeedQuill depends on a judge-model reward optimized <sub>w</sub>ith PPO<sub>,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t th<sub>e rewar</sub>d <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l i<sub>s no</sub>i<sub>sy an</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>rec</sub>t f<sub>or</sub> fi<sub>ne-gra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>l <sub>reca</sub>ll<sub>.</sub> PAPO i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uces v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>l <sub>sens</sub>iti<sub>v</sub>it<sub>y</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h <sub>mas</sub>ki<sub>ng,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t it<sub>s ga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> d<sub>epen</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>rong</sub>l<sub>y on</sub> th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l variant and masking strategy. Object-aware YOLO masking <sub>can</sub> f<sub>ur</sub>th<sub>er</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uce no</sub>i<sub>se w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>ons are</sub> i<sub>ncomp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e</sub> or when masked objects remove contextual information. I<sub>n con</sub>t<sub>ras</sub>t<sub>,</sub> Si<sub>m</sub>L<sub>oss</sub> FFT <sub>prov</sub>id<sub>es a</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>t dif<sub>eren</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> alignment objective between the model’s internal visual <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>rozen</sub> i<sub>mage</sub> <sub>em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ng.</sub>

LLM-judge supervision is a fragile signal. A deeper limit<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> li<sub>n</sub>k<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>wo s</sub>t<sub>ronges</sub>t <sub>non-</sub>Si<sub>m</sub>L<sub>oss approac</sub>h<sub>es.</sub> C<sub>ap-</sub> MAS verifies each proposition with a multimodal judge, and FeedQuill rewards generations with a judge model during PPO; both therefore inherit the reliability of an LLM judge. Recent work shows that LLM judges sufer from a systematic <sub>agreea</sub>bl<sub>eness</sub> bi<sub>as:</sub> th<sub>ey</sub> <sub>accep</sub>t <sub>va</sub>lid <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub>l<sub>mos</sub>t <sub>per</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> (a true-<sub>p</sub>ositive rate near 96%) but fla<sub>g</sub> invalid ones <sub>p</sub>oorl<sub>y</sub> (a true-ne<sub>g</sub>ative rate below 25%), and naive jud<sub>g</sub>e ensembling such as majority voting does not remove this bias (Jain et al. 2025). ## D Prompts

Thi<sub>s sec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> li<sub>s</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>u</sub>ll <sub>se</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f <sub>promp</sub>t<sub>s use</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> thi<sub>s wor</sub>k<sub>, ver-</sub> batim. We <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub> them into (i) the ca<sub>p</sub>tionin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>ts <sub>g</sub>iven to the VLM, (ii) the evaluation <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>ts used to com<sub>p</sub>ute <sub>p</sub>recision, recall, and CLAIR on IIW-400, (iii) the inference-<sub>p</sub>i<sub>p</sub>eline <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>ts of the Ca<sub>p</sub>MAS baseline, and (iv) the reward and judge prompts used by the reward-optimized baselines. Curl<sub>y</sub> braces (e.<sub>g</sub>. {caption}) denote runtime substitutions, and \n denotes a literal newline in a format-tem<sub>p</sub>late prompt. All judge and evaluation calls use greedy decoding (temperature ≈ 0).

## D.1 Captioning Prompts

Single-pass captioner. The single-pass captioning prompt <sub>across a</sub>ll <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s</sub> i<sub>s:</sub>

Describe this image in detail.

CapMAS caption sampling. The CapMAS baseline first <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>es</sub> fi<sub>ve cap</sub>ti<sub>ons per</sub> i<sub>mage</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> VLM <sub>us</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> fi<sub>ve</sub> di<sub>verse</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>a</sub>il<sub>e</sub>d<sub>-</sub>d<sub>escr</sub>i<sub>p</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>promp</sub>t<sub>s:</sub>

1. Describe the given image in a very   
detailed manner.   
2. Provide a detailed description of   
the specified image.   
3. Elaborate on the details of the   
image provided.   
4. Offer an in-depth description of the   
given image.   
5. Thoroughly describe the features of   
the specified image.

## D.2 Evaluation Protocol and Prompts

Precision and recall are computed with a GPT-4o judge. Let G(ˆy) be the atomic propositions extracted from candidate caption yˆ, and let r be the IIW reference description. Define $J ( g ; v , r ) = 1$ when the judge finds proposition g supported by image v and description $r ,$ <sub>an</sub>d 0 <sub>o</sub>th<sub>erw</sub>i<sub>se.</sub> F<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>ow</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> atomic-fact factualit<sub>y</sub> evaluation (Min et al. 2023; Jin<sub>g</sub> et al. 2024), <sub>p</sub>recision is

$$
\mathrm { P r e c i s i o n } ( \hat { y } , v , r ) = \frac { 1 } { \vert \mathcal { G } ( \hat { y } ) \vert } \sum _ { g \in \mathcal { G } ( \hat { y } ) } J ( g ; v , r ) .\tag{15}
$$

## Stage 2 — Merge (LLM).

Based on the following captions describing the same image, create ONE detailed merged caption that incorporates all the important details mentioned across the different captions. Be thorough but avoid repetition. Only describe what is actually mentioned in the captions.

## Stage 3 — Decompose into propositions (LLM).

I want to verify if the given CAPTION is accurate. To assist with this verification, decompose the given CAPTION into atomic propositions. All parts of the caption must be broken down into propositions. The outputs should follow the following format: ’1. proposition one\n2. proposition two\n3. proposition three’. For example, break down ’He is tall, thin, and pale’ into ’1. He is tall.\n2. He is thin.\n3. He is pale.’

## D.4 Reward and Judge Prompts

The FeedQuill PPO baseline first breaks a <sub>g</sub>enerated caption into smaller, atomic information units. A judge scores these <sub>un</sub>it<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o o</sub>bt<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n un</sub>it<sub>-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>prec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on an</sub>d <sub>reca</sub>ll<sub>, an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> h<sub>ar-</sub> monic mean is used as the F1 component R of the reward i<sub>n</sub> S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 4<sub>.</sub>4<sub>.</sub> PAPO i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ea</sub>d <sub>uses a percep</sub>ti<sub>on-cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ency</sub> loss (the KL diver<sub>g</sub>ence between the ca<sub>p</sub>tion distributions on the ori<sub>g</sub>inal and masked ima<sub>g</sub>es) and therefore introduces no additional judge prompt. SimLoss FFT uses a frozen-encoder embedding-alignment objective and SimLoss GRPO uses a black-box ima<sub>g</sub>e–text embeddin<sub>g</sub>-similarit<sub>y</sub> reward (Gemini 2 Embed); neither uses a text judge prompt.

Caption decomposition. The generated caption is decom-<sub>pose</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o s</sub>h<sub>or</sub>t<sub>, s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e-</sub>f<sub>ac</sub>t <sub>propos</sub>iti<sub>ons:</sub>

Decompose the following image caption   
into atomic propositions.   
Rules:   
- Each proposition must be a single,   
verifiable visual fact (one subject +   
one predicate).   
- Keep propositions SHORT (<= 12 words   
each).   
- Do NOT split facts that are   
inherently linked (e.g. "red car on the   
left" -> one prop).   
- Do NOT add information not in the   
caption.   
- Do NOT include vague or subjective   
claims (e.g. "the scene looks   
peaceful").   
- Include spatial relations, colors,   
counts, object states, and text if   
present.   
- Aim for 5-15 propositions depending   
on caption length.   
Caption:   
"{caption}"   
Respond ONLY with a JSON object, no   
markdown, no extra text:   
{{"propositions": ["prop1", "prop2",   
"prop3", ...]}}
